## Document 015: 158円を挟んで膠着するドル円相場 ～中間選挙・イラン・金融政策の_三つの分岐点_が方向性を決める～

Source PDF: all report pdf/158円を挟んで膠着するドル円相場 ～中間選挙・イラン・金融政策の_三つの分岐点_が方向性を決める～.pdf

Investment Navigator

2026 年 10 月 22 日

1/6 
158 円を挟んで膠着するドル円相場 
～中間選挙・イラン・金融政策の"三つの分岐点"が方向性を決める～ 
 経済調査部 シニア・フェロー  嶌峰 義清 
（℡：050-5474-7489）

（要旨） 
○ ドル円相場は、7 月末の日米協調介入、9 月上旬の急激な円高、9 月の日米同時利上げを経て、
10 月に入ってからは1 ドル＝158 円前後の狭いレンジでの推移が続いている。円先物市場に
おける非商業部門の建玉は、9 月前半に過去に例のない規模で円の売り越しから買い越しへ
と転じた。しかし、その後は買い越し幅が縮小し、売建玉が再び増え始めている。投機筋の
円買い意欲は弱まりつつあると言えよう。 
○ 期待インフレ率 （BEI） で実質化した日米の実質金利差は、政策金利・2 年債・10 年債のいず
れでも 2％ポイントを超える米国優位が続いている。名目金利差は縮小したものの、米国の
金利上昇は実質金利の上昇、日本のは期待インフレ率の上昇によるところが大きい。このた
め、実質ベースのドル優位は崩れていない。 
○ 当面の焦点は、①11 月 3 日の米中間選挙、②選挙後のイラン情勢、③日銀の 12 月利上げと
その後のペース、の三つである。民主党が上下両院を制し、イラン情勢が沈静化に向かい、
日銀が利上げ路線を明確にすれば、円高方向への転換が視野に入る。逆の組み合わせとなれ
ば、再び160 円台を試し、介入への警戒が強まる展開も否定できない。

1. 投機筋の円ポジションは"円売り一色"から変化し、158 円を挟んで膠着が続くドル円相場 
ドル円相場は、方向感を欠いた推移が続いている。10 月入り後の値動きは、高値が158.94 円
（10 月14 日） 、安値が156.99 円（10 月2 日）と、月間の値幅は2 円弱にとどまる。 
もっとも、ここに至るまでの道のりは平坦ではなかった。4 月末から5 月の大型連休中には、
160 円台後半まで進んだ円安に対して、政府・日銀が総額11.7 兆円の円買い介入を実施した。そ
れでも円安基調は止まらず、7 月 28 日には163.91 円（ECB参照値）と、約40 年ぶりの円安水準を
つけた。これに対し、7 月 31 日には日米当局が28 年ぶりとなる円買い協調介入に踏み切った。介
入額は 15 兆3993 億円に達し、年初からの累計は27 兆 1342 億円と暦年で過去最大を更新した。 
その後は円高へと自律的な反発も見られた。9 月2～3 日には、日銀の追加利上げ観測の強まり
や米雇用関連指標の下振れを受けてドル円は1 日で3 円以上下落、9 日には153 円台まで円高が進
んだ。この局面では介入実績は無く、ジャクソンホール会議でのウォーシュFRB議長の発言をきっ

### Page 2

2/6 
かけに積み上がったドル買い・円売りの持ち高が、損切りを迫られた結果と考えられる。 
ところが、その後は再び円安に押し戻された。FRBは 9 月 16 日に約3 年ぶりの利上げに踏み切
り、政策金利を3.75～4.00％とした。日銀も18 日に政策金利を1.25％へ引き上げたものの、日
米がそろって利上げしたことで金利差は大きく変わらず、ドル円は158 円台へと戻っている。 
為替市場における"投機的な動き"の指標とされるのが、円先物市場における非商業部門（主に
ヘッジファンドなどとされる）の建玉である。その推移を見ると、ここ数ヶ月で大きな変化が生
じていることがわかる（図表1）。 
6 月 2 日時点の円売建玉は24 万 4416 枚と、円キャリートレードがブームだった2007 年以来の
高水準に達していた。差引（ネット）でも12 万9567 枚の売り越しだった。その後も、9 月1 日時
点では 9 万2227 枚の売り越しと、円売り超の状態が続いていた。 
しかし、9 月前半の急激な円高を境に状況は一変し、9 月 15 日時点では差引が12 万 0359 枚の
買い越しへと転じ、わずか2 週間で円買い方向に21 万枚強も振れた。この振れ幅は、統計をさか
のぼれる1986 年以降で最大である。同時点の円買建玉（23 万 7951 枚）も過去最大を記録した。 
もっとも、その後は買い越し幅の縮小が続いている。10 月13 日時点では、買建玉19 万 1256 枚
に対し売建玉14 万 1049 枚で、差引の買い越しは5 万0207 枚とピークの半分以下に縮んだ。売建
玉は 9 月末の12 万枚台から14 万枚台へと再び増えており、円売りの持ち高が積み上がり始めて
いる兆しもうかがえる。投資主体別に見ると、レバレッジド・ファンドは10 月 13 日時点で3 万
3673 枚の円売り越しとなっており、ヘッジファンドの円買い意欲がすでに後退していることを示
している。 
足元の売建玉は、過去の介入局面のように当局が介入を急がなければならない水準にはない。
一方で、9 月に積み上がった円の買い持ちの解消そのものが、円安圧力として作用している面もあ
ると考えられる。

2.  実質金利差から見たドル円相場
為替相場の方向性を最終的に決めるのは、日米の金融政策と、それを反映した金利差である。
そこで、日米の実質金利差を、政策金利・2 年債・10 年債の3 つの年限で見てみる（図表2） 。こ
こでは、実質化に用いる物価として、市場が織り込む期待インフレ率であるBEI（ブレーク・イー
ブン・インフレ率、10 年債）を用いた。足元の消費者物価は、米国では原油高で押し上げられ、
日本では電気・ガス料金支援で押し下げられており、実勢を見誤りやすいためである。
名目で見ると、政策金利は米FF金利の誘導目標が3.75～4.00％、日本の無担保コール翌日物が
1.25％である。10 月 21 日時点の国債利回りは、米国が2 年債4.79％・10 年債 5.13％、日本が2
年債 1.886％・10 年債2.882％となっている。一方、10 年BEIは米国が2.35％（10 月20 日） 、日
本が 2.1～2.2％程度と、ほぼ同水準である。これを差し引いて実質化すると、筆者の試算ではお

おむね次のようになる。
✓ 政策金利：米国＋1.5～1.6％程度、日本▲0.9～1.0％程度（米国優位・差は約2.5％ポイント）
✓ 2 年債：米国＋2.4％程度、日本▲0.3％程度（米国優位・差は約2.7％ポイント）
✓ 10 年債：米国＋2.8％程度、日本＋0.7％程度（米国優位・差は約2.1％ポイント）
名目で見れば、日本の10 年債利回りが9 月下旬に3.115％と約 30 年ぶりの水準に達するなど、
日米金利差は縮小してきた。しかし、BEIで実質化すると景色は異なる。すべての年限で米国の実
質金利が2％ポイント以上も上回っており、ドル優位の構図はほとんど崩れていないのである。
その背景には、日米で金利上昇の"中身"が異なることがある。米国では、昨年末から長期金利
が約 1％ポイント上昇したが、BEIは 2.25％から2.35％へとほぼ横ばいにとどまった。名目金利の
注：BEIで実質化

4/6 
上昇は、ほぼそのまま実質金利の上昇となり、10 年物の実質利回りは9 月末に 2.93％と2008 年
以来の高水準に達した。これに対し日本では、BEIが過去最高圏の2％台まで上昇しており、名目
金利の上昇の相応の部分を期待インフレ率の上昇が占めている。実際、9 月の日銀の利上げ後も
BEIはむしろ上昇した。日本の金利上昇は"インフレ期待による金利上昇"の色彩が濃く、実質金利
の上昇は限られていると言えよう。 
日本の政策金利は、実質では依然として▲1％近いマイナス圏にあり、金融環境はなお緩和的
だ。日銀の利上げや介入にもかかわらず円安の流れが変わらなかったのは、こうした実質金利差
の構図で説明できる面が大きい。逆に言えば、日本の期待インフレ率が2％近辺で落ち着いたまま
利上げが進むか、米国の実質金利が低下に転じない限り、本格的な円高への転換は難しいと考え
られる。 
なお、日本の物価連動国債は発行残高が約10 兆円と小さく、BEIには流動性の影響も含まれ
る。政策金利や2 年債を10 年BEIで実質化するのも簡便的な手法である点には留意が必要だ。

3.  中間選挙の結果次第で変わるドルの行方 
11 月3 日には米国の中間選挙が行われる。1934 年以降の23 回の中間選挙のうち、現職大統領
の政党が上下両院とも議席を増やしたのはわずか2 回しかない。今回も、ロイター通信の世論調
査ではトランプ大統領の支持率が過去最低の31％まで落ち込み、投票先では民主党47％、共和党
35％と 12 ポイントの差がついている。選挙予測サイトのディシジョン・デスクHQは、民主党が下
院を制する確率を78％、上院を制する確率を56％と見ている。 
結果ごとに、ドル円相場への影響を整理すると次のようになる。 
① 民主党が上下両院を制する場合：予算や減税、関税政策に対する議会のチェックが強まり、
財政拡張への期待が後退することで、米長期金利の低下とドル安につながりやすい。FRBの人事に
対する議会の影響力が強まり、金融政策の独立性への懸念が和らぐことも、長期金利の押し下げ
要因となろう。一方で、予算案を巡る対立が先鋭化すれば、政府機関の閉鎖などを通じたリスク
回避の円買いが生じる可能性もある。いずれにしても、ドル円には下押し圧力がかかる公算が大
きい。 
② 下院は民主党、上院は共和党の"ねじれ"となる場合：現在の政策の大枠が維持される一方
で、新たな大型財政出動は難しくなる。相場の反応は限定的で、関心は再び日米の金融政策へと
移ることになろう。 
③ 共和党が両院の過半数を維持する場合：減税や規制緩和など拡張的な政策への期待から、米
長期金利の上昇とドル高が進みやすい。ドル円が再び160 円台を試し、介入への警戒が一気に高
まる展開も想定される。 
世論調査どおりであれば①ないし②の公算が大きく、ドル円にはどちらかと言えば円高寄りの
材料となろう。ただし、重要なのは選挙後の財政・通商政策や金利見通しの変化であり、選挙直
後の値動きだけで方向性を判断するのは早計だろう。

### Page 5

5/6 
4.  イラン情勢：選挙後の"重し"が外れる 
もう一つの大きな不透明要因がイラン情勢である。2 月 28 日に始まった米国・イスラエルとイ
ランの戦闘は、ホルムズ海峡の事実上の封鎖と、米国によるイラン港湾の海上封鎖を伴ったまま
長期化している。北海ブレント原油先物は20 日に一時1 バレル＝111.99 ドルと約5 ヶ月ぶりの高
値をつけた。 
原油高は、いくつかの経路でドル高円安に作用している。米国では、ガソリン価格の前年比
34％の上昇などを背景に、9 月の消費者物価が同＋3.7％へと加速し、FRBの追加利上げ観測を支え
ている。日本にとっては、エネルギー輸入額の増加を通じて貿易収支が悪化し、実需の円売りが
増える。加えて、地政学リスクの高まりは"有事のドル買い"も誘いやすい。 
注目されるのは、トランプ大統領が「中間選挙前にはイランを攻撃しない」と明言している点
だ。言い換えれば、選挙後はこの制約が外れることになる。選挙後に軍事行動が再開されれば、
原油価格の一段高と米利上げ観測の強まりを通じて、ドル高円安が進むリスクがある。逆に、仲
介国を通じた交渉がまとまり、海峡の再開に向かえば、原油価格の下落とともに米長期金利が低
下し、ドル安円高へと振れやすい。実際、20～21 日には原油安を受けて米長期金利が低下し、ド
ルが売られる場面が見られた。

5.  日米の金融政策：12 月の"同時利上げ"の先が焦点 
FRBは9 月に約3 年ぶりの利上げに踏み切り、年内にもう1 回の利上げを示唆した。10 月14 日
に9 月の消費者物価が前年比3.7％と予想を上回ると、27～28 日のFOMCでの利上げ確率は一時5
割前後まで上昇した。しかし、17 日にウォーシュ議長が10 月会合の判断を予断しない姿勢を示し
たことで、利上げ確率は1 割台半ばまで低下している。市場では「10 月は見送り、12 月に利上
げ」との見方が大勢となった。一部には、12 月が今回の利上げ局面で最後になるとの予想も出て
いる。 
一方、日銀の植田総裁は、9 月の利上げ後に「政策の局面は変化した」と述べ、その後も基調的
な物価上昇率が2％に「かなり近づいている」との認識を示した。そのうえで、 「これ以上上がる
ことは容認できない」とも踏み込んでいる。29～30 日の決定会合では政策金利が据え置かれる公
算が大きいが、基調物価が2％に到達したと判断されると報じられている。 
日米がともに12 月に利上げすれば、名目の政策金利差は変わらない。為替にとって重要なの
は、その先の方向性の違いである。FF金利が明確に中立水準を超えるFRBの利上げが最終局面に近
づく一方、日銀は中立金利を目指して利上げを続ける。この構図が鮮明になれば、日米の実質金
利差は徐々に縮小に向かおう。逆に、原油高を受けて米国の物価上昇が加速し、FRBが一段の利上
げを迫られる展開となれば、実質金利差の縮小は見込めず、ドル円相場の膠着状態を演出しよ
う。

6.  今後の見通し：年末に向けて緩やかな円高を想定、ただし分岐は大きい 
以上を踏まえると、当面のドル円相場は、日米の金融政策会合と中間選挙という大きなイベン

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
6/6 
トを控え、当面は現状の狭いレンジでの推移が続くと考えられる。10 月27～28 日のFOMCは据え置
きが市場の大勢だ。FOMCで利上げが見送られ、その後の日銀会合で基調物価2％到達が確認されて
12 月利上げへの地ならしが進めば、円高方向に振れやすい組み合わせとなる。しかし、市場では
FRBも年内利上げを実施するとの見方が強く、ドル円相場を動かす材料とはなりにくい。逆に、
FRBが10 月のFOMCで利上げを決定した場合、ドル円相場は159 円を超える円安進展の可能性が高
まる。 
年末にかけては、①中間選挙で民主党が議会の主導権を握り、②イラン情勢が交渉による解決
に向かい原油価格が落ち着き、③日銀が12 月に利上げを実施して来年以降も利上げ継続の姿勢を
示す、という三つの条件がそろえば、日米の実質金利差は縮小に向かう。その場合は、150 円台半
ばから前半への円高が進む可能性がある。投機筋の売建玉が再び積み上がっている局面であれ
ば、その巻き戻しが円高の勢いを増幅することも考えられる。 
一方で、①共和党の善戦、②選挙後のイランへの軍事行動の再開、③FRBの利上げ継続＋日銀の
利上げ慎重姿勢打ち出し、といった組み合わせとなれば、ドル円は再び160 円台を試すことにな
ろう。その場合、政府・日銀は過去の介入水準である160 円台前半から164 円近辺を意識しつ
つ、円安のスピードを抑えるための介入に踏み切る公算が大きい。ただし、これまでの経験が示
すように、ファンダメンタルズが変わらない中での介入は"時間を買う"政策にすぎず、効果が一
時的にとどまる可能性には注意が必要だ。 
筆者は、①米中間選挙は最低でも上下院のねじれ、場合によっては上院においても民主党の過
半数獲得、②選挙後に米国がイランへの軍事行動を再開、同時に仲介国を介した交渉を再開する
ものの、膠着状態が続く、③日米とも利上げ継続、をメインシナリオとしている。 
さらにリスク要因として、①米中間選挙の結果がなかなか確定せず、議会勢力の確定に時間が
かかる、②イラン情勢はフーシ派から攻撃を受けているサウジアラビアや、総選挙を終えたイス
ラエルによる本格的な攻撃参戦、の可能性を挙げる。 
このように不透明要因が多いことも足元のドル円相場の膠着の要因になっていると考えられる
が、短期的な値動きに一喜一憂するよりも、実質金利差という基本的な決定要因がどちらに動い
ているかを冷静に見極めることが重要であり、日米中央銀行の金融政策姿勢が最も注目される。

以 上

### Page 3

Genius Electronic Optical (3406.TW)
27 October 2026 Citi Research
3
EBO: Solving AI Data Centers’ Connectivity
Bottlenecks
Expanded Beam Optical (EBO) technology uses lens arrays to expand and collimate
light between optical fibers, enabling transmission across a contactless interface.
Compared with conventional physical-contact connectors, EBO reduces sensitivity
to dust, cleaning requirements and connector wear, while enabling faster
installation and higher connection density. These benefits become increasingly

valuable as AI clusters require more fiber connections in difficult-to-access
locations, including optical backplanes. For Genius, this creates an opportunity to
extend its precision-optics expertise into infrastructure where reliability and
deployment efficiency directly affect customer economics.
Figure 1. 3M – EBO product demo Figure 2. 3M – EBO product demo
© 2026 Citigroup Inc. No redistribution without Citigroup’s written permission. © 2026 Citigroup Inc. No redistribution without Citigroup’s written permission.

Source: Citi Research, 3M Source: Citi Research, 3M
Technology Requirements and Genius’s Manufacturing
Advantage
EBO relaxes some connector-interface tolerances, but manufacturing its optical
structures still requires precise lens geometry, consistent fiber-to-lens positioning,
dimensional stability and stringent optical-loss control. The competitive challenge
is reproducing that performance at high volumes with attractive yields. Genius’
established precision-optics manufacturing capabilities and production scale

therefore complement 3M’s proprietary ferrule technology, materials expertise and
customer relationships. Their collaboration supports commercialization and
capacity expansion, while 3M’s planned approximately 4.7% equity investment
strengthens strategic alignment. In our view, this provides Genius with a credible
route into optical connectivity, given its smartphone-lens expertise translates into
qualified, reliable production.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 4

Genius Electronic Optical (3406.TW)
27 October 2026 Citi Research
4
Implications for Genius: Strategic Diversification, with
Earnings Following the Ramp
The partnership positions Genius as a manufacturing participant in 3M’s EBO
expansion, offering a potential growth engine beyond consumer-device optics. Our
interpretation is that technology qualification, production yields and capacity
readiness will matter most initially, with stronger revenue potential as deployments

accelerate from 2027. We estimate the revenue contribution to reach about
8%/28% in 2027 /2028 assuming around 30m/140m units based on 3M’s
addressable market estimate.
Muted smartphone outlook well anticipated, more optical
upside in AI
Genius’ near-term momentum is still highly relying on smartphone order flow. Its
peer, Largan, has indicated a muted outlook for the smartphone industry as the
peak production ramp for newly launched models has passed; we expect Genius’

revenue to decline seasonally in 4Q26. However, another wave of base-model
smartphone launches in 1Q27 should help support demand during what is typically
its seasonally slower period at the start of next year. Given its steady market share
gain, we expect a stable GM ahead. While Genius is also navigating the
opportunities in CPO and PMLA for FAU, we believe it takes time and the EBO
contribution will provide more substantial upside for the next two years. We

slightly lower our earnings projection for 2026/2027 but lift 2028 estimate by 6%,
given better visibility for its cooperation with 3M. We raise our TP to NT$1,380 from
NT$1,050 previously (higher PER of 33x of our 2027 /28 EPS projection vs 25x
previously). 33x PER is above the high end of its 5-year historical range as we
believe the high growth optical opportunity in AI arena and 3M's strategic
cooperation should further reinforce Genius' position in capturing the expanding

optical connector markets, leading to better growth and re-rating potential for
Genius.

### Page 8

Genius Electronic Optical (3406.TW)
27 October 2026 Citi Research
8
Genius Electronic Optical
Company description
Genius is a leading lens provider. It was established in 1989 and listed on
TWSE in 2005. Its revenue increased from NT$4.5bn in 2010 to NT$23.2bn in
2024. The revenue breakdown for Genius in 2023 was 73% smartphone lens
and 27% others.
Investment strategy
We have a Buy rating on Genius. We expect Genius to see resilient sales
momentum driven by stable lens sales in iPhone business. Moreover, we see

its new development in EBO business to provide strong upside potential in
the longer term.
Valuation
Our target price for Genius of NT$1,380 is based on 33x 2027 /28 EPS. We are
using 2027 /28 as the valuation base year as we believe the new business in
optical connector will be a new catalyst starting from next year. 33x PER is
above the high end of its 5-year historical range as we believe the high growth
optical opportunity in AI arena will lead better growth and re-rating and 3M's

strategic cooperation further reinforces Genius' position in capturing the
expanding optical connector markets. Genius used to trade based on
smartphone product cycle and the growth has been stagnant. With the new
optical connector opportunity, we view it should be resuming strong growth
ahead.
Risks
Key downside risks that could mean the stock fails to achieve our target price
include: lower-than-expected smartphone sell-through; lower iPhone share

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

M
Update
Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 GM Dragged by Inventory
Write-off
Despite the secular tailwinds of increasing power consumption
in AI clusters, we think near-term sentiment toward Delta could
remain lackluster post weaker 3Q26 results.
3Q26 earnings fell short on lower GM:
• Off revenue of NT$197,059mn (+8% QoQ/+31% YoY), GM contracted  by
1.6ppt QoQ to 34.1% (vs. MSe/consensus at 35.7%/36.2%), which was
dragged 2ppt lower by the inventory write-off

• Opex was well controlled at NT$34,535mn (opex ratio 17.5%), lower than our
estimate of NT$36,983mn (opex ratio 18.4%) and consensus of NT$37,129mn
(opex ratio 18.0%)
• Operating profit came in at NT$32,620mn (+7% QoQ/+31% YoY; OPM
16.6%), 7% lower  than MSe of NT$34,992mn (OPM 17.4%) and 13% lower
than consensus of NT$37,677mn (OPM 18.2%).
• With non-op gains of NT$1,046mn (including FX gain of NT$415mn), net
profit was NT$23,495mn (-7% QoQ/+26% YoY; EPS NT$9.04), 8%/15% short

of MSe/consensus at NT$25,554mn (EPS NT$9.84)/NT$27,611mn (EPS NT
$10.40).
Our view:
• We believe near-term sentiment toward the stock will stay soft, though the
weak financial performance in 3Q26 was mainly impacted by component
supply tightness
• We believe Delta should grow its revenue by 10% QoQ with margin
expansion in 4Q26, and will continue to benefit from the increasing power
consumption of AI clusters in 2027
• Earnings call at 3pm HKT on Oct 29th  (details here)

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey. Voting
opens 10 November 2026.

Morgan Stanley Research 3
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28 EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 63% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth

n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 3

Delta Electronics (2308.TW)
29 October 2026 Citi Research
3
3Q Result Miss, Expecting Recovery in 4Q26
Delta’s 3Q earnings and GM were below expectation due to component shortages
and delays in data center deployment. GM declined 1.6ppts QoQ to 34.1%,
primarily reflecting inventory provisions equivalent to 1.7% of revenue, driven by
supply chain mismatches, customer delivery postponements, and product
specification adjustments. Nevertheless, OPM remained resilient at 16.6%, broadly

flat QoQ and YoY, supported by operating leverage.
Management indicated that AI-related businesses across its four divisions now
account for approximately 50% of consolidated revenue, highlighting Delta’s
increasing exposure to AI infrastructure investment. Management remains
confident in Delta’s competitive positioning, particularly its integrated capabilities
spanning power conversion, power distribution, thermal management, and
infrastructure solutions. Although the industry continues to face component

shortages, especially for certain power semiconductor devices, Delta believes its
supply chain management capabilities compare favorably to competitors. The
company also plans to establish a major manufacturing facility in Penang,
Malaysia, leveraging the region’s established infrastructure supply chain and
engineering talent.
Figure 1. Delta - 3Q26 earnings review
© 2026 Citigroup Inc. No redistribution without Citigroup’s written permission.
Source: Company Reports, Citi Research Estimates, Bloomberg consensus

HVDC Adoption and SOFC Commercialization Create
Additional Long- T erm Growth Opportunities
Delta expects the transition toward high-voltage DC power architecture to
accelerate as AI rack power density continues to increase. The company began
shipping ±400V HVDC solutions in 3Q26, while NVIDIA is promoting 800VDC
architectures for next-generation AI infrastructure. Management believes HVDC
adoption will eventually become widespread given its potential to improve power

conversion efficiency and reduce distribution losses, although standardization,
installation requirements, and shortages of qualified electrical workers could
constrain the near-term rollout. Beyond power conversion, Delta is developing
solid oxide fuel cell (SOFC) solutions to address growing demand for distributed
power generation. The company plans to begin commercial production in 2027,
with an initial manufacturing line providing approximately 100MW of annual

capacity, primarily supporting customer qualification and pilot installations. Initial

customers include domestic utility and industrial users, alongside several overseas
customers. Management emphasized that SOFC shipments will remain limited

4Q26 Recovery Expected as Inventory Normalizes, with
Positive Growth Outlook for 2027
Management expects revenue to increase sequentially in 4Q26 as delayed
customer deliveries resume and inventory begins to normalize. Gross margin

should improve from 3Q26 levels, although management remained cautious given
potential year-end inventory adjustments. The 3Q26 inventory provisions largely
reflected customer-specific products rather than structural obsolescence, and
some provisions could potentially be reversed following successful deliveries.
Nevertheless, management acknowledged that inventory buffers will likely remain
structurally higher than historical levels to accommodate volatile AI infrastructure

deployment schedules. Raw material costs have increased approximately 2% year-
to-date, prompting ongoing pricing negotiations with customers, with limited
recovery expected in 4Q26 and a more meaningful impact in 2027. Operating
expenses will continue increasing in absolute terms to support R&D and capacity
expansion. Management remains positive on 2027 revenue growth, although it did
not provide quantitative guidance. Separately, management expects no near-term

shipment disruption from the ongoing Vicor intellectual property litigation.
We lower earnings projection on power shortages, labor constraints, financing
conditions, and regulatory uncertainties could create quarterly shipment volatility,
but we believe its multiyear AI infrastructure growth cycle intact. We maintain Buy
with TP NT$1,715 (based on lower PER of 35x of 2027 EPS, from 40x previously)

### Page 7

Delta Electronics (2308.TW)
29 October 2026 Citi Research
7
Delta Electronics
Company description
Delta was established in 1975 and is the world's leading computer switching
power supply manufacturer. Its business is divided into: 1) Power Electronics
(59% of 2022 revenue), including Switching Power Supply, Components,
Display and EV Solution; 2) Infrastructure (27% of revenue), including Wind
power, Telecom power, UPS, EV Infrastructure, Networking; 3) Automation

(14% of revenue), including Industrial Automation (IA), Building Automation;
and 4) Others ( <1% of revenue).
Investment strategy
We rate Delta Electronics shares as Buy due to its strong execution and the
ability to expand margin through favorable product mix shift to AI power and
thermal solutions. We believe EV business slowdown is in the price, and we
expect margins to improve driven by AI solution spec upgrades and expanding
business scale. Delta is now positioning itself as an infrastructurer enabler

rather than a pure component supplier, which deepens its key position in the
AI supply chain.
Valuation
Our target price for Delta Electronics of NT$1,715 is based on 35x 2027E EPS
estimate. Our target multiple is set at the higher end of its trading range since
2023, which we believe is warranted by Delta’s strong earnings growth and
better product mix prospects. We also believe the company’s leading industry
position, long product development cycle, and strong engagement with

customers should translate into high orders/earnings visibility.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price
are: 1) lower contribution from non-PC businesses; 2) products in the new
businesses (LED lighting, electric vehicle, and Voice) growing more slowly
than expected; and 3) PC power supply losing market share.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

聯亞(3081.TT) 
產業：電子–通信網路 
矽光需求持續強勁，更多設備投產與新增後段外包夥伴帶動產能倍增  
 投資評價與建議 
受惠CW Laser出貨量持續增加、新購顯影設備逐步進駐以及後段代工廠產能
開出，預估稅後EPS為21.01/49.3/97.15元，考量NPO和CPO技術逐步成熟且
聯亞具備多波⻑雷射領先技術，從2028年獲利評價，給予40倍本益比。 
 重點評論分析 
矽光產品出貨增加 ， 3Q26營收成⻑ ， 主要客⼾已下⻑期訂單 ， 4Q26營收攀升 ：
3Q26營收季增27.9%至15.6億元 ， 係因矽光產品出貨增加 ， 毛利率因營收規模
擴大上升1.11ppts至58.6%，營業費用較2Q26增加約1,400萬元至1.43億元，
主要來自研發費用以及隨獲利增加按比例提列之員工分紅費用，稅後EPS為
6.12元。4Q26矽光產品需求持續強勁，主要客戶皆已開始下6個⽉以上的⻑期
訂單，訂單能見度佳，隨公司本身與下游代工廠產能逐步開出，預期矽光產品
出貨量逐季成⻑，預估4Q26營收季增23.5%至19.3億元，稅後EPS為7.19元。  
設備與後段代工產能開出， 2027和2028年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%：展
望2027~2028年，1.6T矽光產品已量產，3.2T與NPO/CPO應用之更高功率矽
光產品則和客戶持續合作開發和驗證中 ， 針對特定客戶之資料中心200G PD已
完成驗證且磊晶產能已規劃，預計未來數⽉將開始量產。目前E-Beam、奈米
壓印技術與DUV深紫外光微影三種技術皆同步進行擴產，隨更多E-Beam投
產，1Q27產能將明顯增加，奈米壓印設備預計於2026年底進廠。 
 投資風險 
設備與後段產能開出進度不如預期。

2

| 營運概況  
矽光產品出貨持續增加與後段產能開出，3Q26營收成⻑，主要客⼾已下⻑期訂單，4Q26營收持續攀升： 3Q26營收季
增27.9%至15.6億元 ， 係因矽光產品出貨量增加 ， 毛利率因營收規模擴大上升1.11ppts至58.6% ， 營業利益為7.71億元，
營業利益率為49.4%，營業費用較2Q26增加約1,400萬元至1.43億元，主要來自研發費用以及隨獲利增加按比例提列之
員工分紅費用，折舊費用較2Q26增加160萬元至7,660萬元，稅後淨利為6.21億元，稅後EPS為6.12元。4Q26矽光產品
需求持續強勁，主要客戶皆已開始下6個⽉以上的⻑期訂單，訂單能見度佳，隨公司本身與下游代工廠產能逐步開出，
預期矽光產品出貨量逐季成⻑，預估4Q26營收季增23.5%至19.3億元，稅後EPS為7.19元。影響毛利率因素有三：整體
營收與產出、匯率變化以及成本。管理層預期營收於接下來幾季將逐季成⻑，惟3Q26前半段台幣升值，隨後9⽉底台幣
轉貶值，匯率變化對3Q26毛利率產生些許不利影響，加上更多人員召募以及設備折舊成本逐步反映，且產品訂價方面，
聯亞僅維持賺取合理利潤，將無法持續大幅漲價，導致毛利率於接下來幾季成⻑幅度將趨緩。 
設備與後段代工產能開出，2027 和 2028 年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%：展望 2027~2028 年，聯亞 1.6T 矽光產品
已經開始量產，3.2T 與 NPO/CPO 應用之更高功率矽光產品則和客戶持續合作開發和驗證中，另外過去受限產能，公
司設備主要用於生產 LD，自 2027 年開始，針對特定客戶之資料中心 200G 以上規格之 PD 已完成驗證，並已規劃相對
應磊晶產能，將於未來數⽉將開始量產，惟出貨量仍遠小於 LD。2026年 8 ⽉董事會決議與 1 美系資料中心客戶簽訂為
期 4 年之⻑期供貨合約，為滿足該客戶之需求，公司追加約 30 億元資本支出，該資本支出購置之設備可帶動 2027 和
2028 年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%，另外因應磷化銦基板缺料問題，聯亞目前與 2 家日本主要供應商與 1 家美國主
要供應商達成未來數年的⻑期供貨合約，且美系客戶亦提供部分資金，協助確保後續大規模量產之基板供應無虞。因應
產能不足，聯亞持續新增後段外包合作夥伴位，台灣地區增加數家大型集團作為合作夥伴，該集團在光通訊領域投入意
願高，其資本支出承諾與實力遠高於早期合作夥伴，有助於與聯亞共同爭取國際 AI 訂單。曝光顯影製程方面，目前 E-
Beam、奈米壓印技術與 DUV 深紫外光微影三種技術皆同步進行擴產，首部新購置之 E-Beam 設備已於 2026 年 7 ⽉
開始量產，帶動 7 ⽉營收大幅躍升，隨更多 E-Beam 與 MOCVD 設備投產，1Q27 產能將有明顯增加且未來 8 個季度
產能將逐步增加 ， 奈米壓印設備預計於 2026 年底進場 ， DUV 深紫外光微影設備則於 2027 年以後加入生產 。 綜上所述 ，
預估 2026/2027/2028 年營收年增 155%/130%/86%至56.2/129.1/239.5 億元，稅後 EPS 為 21.01/49.3/97.15 元。

## Document 200: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung

### Page 1

기업분석 2026.10.30 www.daishin.com

삼성전자 
(005930) 
 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것 
류형근

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 500,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
231,000 
반도체업종

◼ 3분기 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 
◼ 주주환원은 시점의 문제일 뿐. 강화 방향성은 여전히 유효. 
◼ 메모리반도체 이익의 폭증이 가격의 매력을 유발. 비중확대 전략 유효.

매력적인 가격, 변하지 않은 주주환원 정책의 방향성 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 메모리반도체 주도 폭발적 이익 상승이 가격 (주
가)의 매력을 지속 유발하고 있고, 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향성에도 변한 것
은 없다는 판단. 절대적으로 싼 주가를 비중 확대의 기회로 삼을 필요. 
1. 주주환원, 시점의 문제 
금번 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 대외 불확실성이 큰 만큼, 
시장은 자사주 매입/소각과 같은 즉각적 주가 부양 정책의 발표를 기대.  
당사는 삼성전자의 주주환원이 “시점의 문제”라 생각. 총 주주환원의 규모도, 
차기 (2027-2029년) 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향에도 변한 것은 없다는 판
단. 시장이 기대하는 자사주 매입/소각은 우선주 중심으로 시행될 가능성이 
높으며, 시행 시, 그 규모는 25조원 내외일 것 (DPS 12,567원)으로 전망. 
2. 메모리반도체, 더 올라갈 것 
보수적 환율 전망 (2027년 평균 환율 1,300원 적용)에도, 2027년 메모리반도
체 영업이익은 608조원에 달할 것. 이익 전망의 상향 여력 또한 유효. 
1) 최대 생산, 최대 판매 
삼성전자는 금일 4Q26 DRAM 판매량 가이던스로 전분기 대비 10% 중후반 
성장 (q-q +18%로 추정)을 제시. 일부 투자자들은 높아진 기저 부담으로 
2027년 판매 성장이 제한될 것을 우려. 당사는 높아진 기저에도 2027년 
DRAM 판매가 전년대비 20% 상승할 것으로 전망. 
- 극단적 수급 불균형 속, 삼성전자는 “최대 생산, 최대 판매” 정책을 시행 
중. 삼성전자만의 강력한 Execution이 20% 대 성장을 유지시킬 원동력. 
- DRAM 1c 수율의 조기 개선 속, 범용 DRAM 양산에 DRAM 1c Capa가 일
부 투입될 것으로 예상. Bit 성장을 높일 수 있는 변화가 될 것. 
2) 열려 있는 가격의 상방 
1Q27 모바일 DRAM에서 Surprise가 발생할 것이라는 의견을 유지 (전분기 
대비 30% 이상 상승). 장기계약이 체결된 CSP향으로도 가격의 추가 상승 여
력이 존재한다는 점을 상기할 필요. CSP의 경우, 당장의 비용 부담보다 AI 시
장 선점을 우선시. 장기계약으로 형성된 가격 Band의 천장 (Ceiling) 또한 시
장 우려 대비 낮지 않음을 감안할 필요. 2027년 범용 DRAM ASP는 전년대비 
40% 중후반까지 상승할 가능성 (현 전망: 전년대비 34% 상승).  
3) HBM, 제품 경쟁력과 점유율의 동반 성장  
최근 삼성전자향으로 HBM4/HBM4e 추가 공급 요청이 쇄도하고 있는 것으
로 추정. 일부 고객은 연내 추가 공급 시 2027년 계약 가격을 선제 지급할 
의사를 표출. 제품 경쟁력이 회복되었음을 보여주는 증거라 생각. 
제품 경쟁력의 회복과 최대 Capa의 이점 속, 2027년은 HBM 점유율 1위를 
확보하는 기념비적 해가 될 것으로 전망 (매출액 기준 점유율 38% 예상).

KOSPI 5937.66

## Document 223: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름삼성바이오로직스(207940) 환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름삼성바이오로직스(207940) 환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름 - 기업.pdf

 절대 (달러환산) (7.9) 11.8  (4.8)

3Q26Pre: 환율 하락에도 성장 흐름 유지 
매출액 1조 3,477억원(+7.2%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 5,957억원(-6.0%, OPM 
44.2%)로 컨센서스에 부합할 것으로 전망. 3분기 원달러 평균 환율은 1,423원으로 전분기 
평균 환율 대비 약 5.2% 하락에도 불구하고 성장 흐름 지속. 
2분기 노조 파업으로 인한 영향은 3분기 생산을 진행했으며 4분기 매출에 반영될 예정으로 
연간 실적에 미치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상. GSK로부터 인수한 록빌 공장도 안정적
으로 생산이 이루어지고 있으며 CAPA 여유분은 추가 수주를 진행 중으로 28~29년 이후 
완전 가동 전망. 
4Q, 6공장 건설과 폴리펩타이드 인수 
유상 증자 관련 투자 설명서 상 투자 계획에 따르면 6공장 투자는 26년부터 29년까지 진행 
될 예정으로 29년 가동 목표. 6공장은 5공장과 동일한 18만 리터 규모로 건설되며, 연내 투
자 집행을 시작함에 따라 건설 발표도 임박한 것으로 추정. 동사는 일정 수준 이상 기존 공
장 수주를 확보한 후 후속 공장 건설을 진행해오고 있어 non-binding 수주 계약의 본계약 
전환도 순차적으로 이루어질 것으로 전망. 
폴리펩타이드 인수도 현재 공개 매수를 진행 중에 있으며 연내 마무리될 것으로 예상. 
1H26 폴리펩타이드 매출은 2.37억 유로(+41.6%)로 GLP-1 agonist 등 펩타이드 의약품 
수요 증가에 따라 높은 성장 지속. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원으로 하향 
매수 의견을 유지하며 목표 주가는 기존 210만원에서 200만원으로 하향. 목표 주가 하향은 
유상 증자에 따른 EPS 희석에 기인. 글로벌 항체 CDMO 수요가 여전히 견조하며, 폴리펩
타이드 인수로 펩타이드로의 확장에 따른 추가적인 성장 기대. 주주 대상 유상 증자 영향과 
파업 우려 등이 있으나 노사 합의 시 주가 반등 전망.

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Oil Price Dynamics Remain Key to the Outlook
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Inflation, interest rates and equity market dynamics have changed materially since mid-
August, as widening attacks on Middle East and Russian crude oil and refining capacity
worsened supply and price conditions. Indeed, with the oil market already strained, an
early-September attack on a major Saudi Arabian export pipeline sharply raised concerns

about a protracted and deepening oil crisis. While Middle East crude exports eventually
managed to significantly rebound, increased security risks and freight costs raised
physical crude prices.
Against this backdrop, the spot Brent price surged from $92/barrel in mid-August
back into a $115 to $135/barrel price range until recently. Reflecting unusual market
tightness, other North Sea oil grades were recently trading even higher, up to $160/
barrel, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). Even as it cooled from $135

to $115/barrel between October 2 and October 20, spot Brent is still almost double
year-ago levels, and so are many diesel prices. The AAA average U.S. retail diesel price
recently hit a new record and was 74% higher year-over-year (YoY) as of October 20.
With insufficient regional supply, the New York Harbor wholesale diesel price is 130%
higher from a year ago, according to the Energy Information Administration (EIA).
Indeed, sharply higher oil prices and a growing diesel shortage have put the global diesel

market under extreme strain. The U.S., for example, has little distillate cushion and
limited spare refining capacity. The distillate inventory (diesel and fuel oil) has decreased
from 37 days of demand coverage in January to just 26 days by early October, according
to the EIA. This 30% drop brought the supply buffer to the bottom of its historical
range. With limited refining capacity and reliance on imports for about 10% of regional
consumption, East Coast distillate inventories are the lowest since 1990, for example,

and are expected to remain below their 5-year average into late 2027, according to EIA
data. More broadly, according to the IEA’s October Oil Market Report (OMR), “oil market
balances are forecast to remain in deficit through the end of the year, with constrained
middle distillate supplies likely to persist well into 2027.”
Thus, although wage growth/labor demand do not appear inflationary, the oil crisis
risks continuing to feed “core” inflation. According to the Federal Reserve’s Beige Book 1

covering precisely the mid-August to early-October period of heightened oil-market
turmoil, “higher oil prices permeated all District reports,” with firms noting that fuel
surcharges were passing on increased costs throughout the supply chain. The 22% rise
in airfares over the past year alone contributed around 0.2 percentage point (pp) to the
2.6% 12-month increase in the “core” consumer price index (CPI) for September. Aside
from transportation costs effects, the spike in diesel prices also portends high food

prices with a few-month lag given planting/harvesting cost considerations.
By solidifying expectations for higher Fed interest rates to fight inflation, the surge in oil
prices has contributed to the spike in long-term Treasury yields this year. According to an
October 13, 2026, Empirical Research Partners report, the Iran-war oil crisis is estimated
to have contributed about 0.50 pp to the roughly 2.40 pp increase in the real U.S. long-
term Treasury rate from 2020 to early October (Exhibit 1A). According to the report, this

oil-price effect accounts for “much of the rise in real rates since February 27, 2026...”
On the positive side, while most other underpinnings for higher rates compared to the
pre-pandemic decade are structural (Exhibit 1A), the oil-price-related effect on interest
rates is reversible, implying that a resolution to the current oil crisis has the potential to
help lower both inflation and interest rates, relieving some of the current headwinds to
growth and asset prices.

Indeed, while much of the increase in interest rates since the pandemic reflects stronger,
more normal economic growth, and has thus been accompanied by surging corporate
1 Summary of Commentary on Current Economic Conditions by Federal Reserve District, Federal Reserve, October 2026.
Investment Implications
Oil market conditions remain a swing
factor for the outlook. A shift lower in
oil risk premia and improvement on
the refining front would go a long way

toward easing inflation and interest
rates while bolstering risk-asset
appetite. A gradual increase in Fixed
Income portfolio duration continues to
make sense, in our view.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
earnings and risk-asset prices, worsening oil-market conditions since mid-summer and
associated spike in yields have been accompanied by a loss of momentum in U.S. equity
prices (Exhibit 1B). Given their higher sensitivity to energy price and interest rates shocks,
Small-caps lost relatively more momentum than Large-caps during this period.
The Large-cap rally also faded, though, as oil and interest rate headwinds widened stock-

level return dispersion, with roughly 40% of constituent stocks in bear market territory
(down 20% or more). Still, buoyed by the Technology sector as well as by expectations
of conflict resolution, the market-capitalization weighted S&P 500 Index has mainly
consolidated just below its peak. While the equal-weighted S&P 500 Index stumbled more,
it was just about 3% off its peak as of October 20.
While the economy remains resilient and profit expectations remain strong, persistent

oil market turmoil makes the macro backdrop more fragile. For example, the Beige Book
not only highlights concern about spreading fuel-related inflation, as noted above, but its
language also suggests that economic activity lost some momentum since its mid-July
to late-August report. Overall, it describes growth as slight to moderate, with generally
moderate manufacturing growth except for robust activity in the energy-heavy Dallas
District. Little growth is reported in other sectors overall, with most Districts seeing little

to no change in consumer spending, for example.
Employment was described as stable to little changed, with generally adequate labor supply
except for certain specialized trade workers. Five of twelve Districts note some reports of
businesses cutting staff through attrition, delaying hiring or anticipating less job creation
as a result of AI-driven productivity gains. Most Districts reported that business maintained
a positive outlook for coming months, though “expectations tend to be for slight growth,

weighted toward manufacturing and burdened by persistent uncertainty.”
The September employment and retail sales reports seem to validate the anecdotal Beige
Book evidence. Also, the 0.4% annualized manufacturing production growth over the July to
September period (and 1.1% YoY, according to the Federal Reserve Broad), while welcome,
is hardly an indication of booming conditions. Against a backdrop of rising electricity prices,
wage growth lagging inflation, and higher gasoline prices broadly offsetting this year’s tax

cut benefits, real consumer spending is unlikely to accelerate much absent stronger labor
demand and real wage growth. A resolution to the oil crisis that brings economic relief and
tempers interest rates is thus critical for continued expansion in 2027, in our view.

Investment Implications
Portfolio maintenance is a key
component of successful investing.
It’s time for investors to assess their
portfolios—or look under the hood.
MARKET VIEW
Six Questions Investors Should Be Asking Themselves Right Now
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
After four years of rising equity prices, investors have plenty to celebrate. Despite

inflation scares, geopolitical tensions, trade wars, rising interest rates and breathless
predictions around AI, the S&P 500 has returned some 131% (total returns) since the
bull market in equities commenced in October 2022. Corporate earnings hav e generally
exceeded expectations; AI has ignited a historic investment boom, and America’s
technology giants have powered the market to new heights.
We believe the bull will rumble on into 2027. We also believe that now is the time for

investors to take stock of their portfolios, since bull markets have a way of changing the
composition of portfolios. In addition, investors should not assume that the investment
strategies that worked well in the past four years will necessarily deliver comparable
results during the next four. Below are six questions investors should be asking
themselves right now:
Number One: How concentrated is my portfolio? Many investors believe they are
invested in the stock market when, in fact, they are invested in a handful of companies—

specifically AI-related names that have soared and dominated the index over the past
few years. Information technology now accounts for roughly 40% of the market cap of
the S&P 500, while the top 10 companies represent a share of 38.4%.
As the Financial Times recently noted, U.S. equity and credit markets have become so
increasingly concentrated in AI-related groups that “more than tw o-thirds of companies
in the Russell 1000 index of large U.S. groups are linked to AI, either directly or

indirectly or because the technology is becoming embedded in their business models.” 2
Meanwhile, Big Technology hyperscalers and companies that are beneficiaries of the AI
spending boom make up about 16% of the U.S. high-grade bond market after a huge
borrowing binge, making the grouping the largest in the investment-grade bond market. 2
The upshot: Investors that have been rewarded by concentration need to ask themselves
what percentage of their wealth is tied to the all-encompassing AI trade and consider

what would happen if mega-cap tech leadership falters.
Number Two: How diversified is my portfolio? While concentration asks whether
investors own too much of a few assets, diversification asks whether investors own enough
of different assets that behave differently when economic cycles or financial conditions
change. A portfolio containing hundreds of U.S. Equities via exchange-traded funds, mutual
funds and other assets may appear diversified. But if those companies are overwhelmingly

exposed to the same economic cycle, interest rate environment, or technology theme,
the diversification benefits are diluted. Case in point: While we remain constructive on
Emerging Market (EM) Equities, investors need to understand that a significant driver of
EM returns has come from AI-driven markets like Taiwan and South Korea.
Today, true diversification means considering exposure to hard assets (commodities, real
estate), EM mining and agricultural leaders, EM bonds and currencies, China bio-technology,

Small-cap international, and Private Equity and Hedge Funds for qualified investors.
Number Three: How tax-efficient is my portfolio? Bull markets create wealth—in
addition to tax liabilities. The good news is that after four years of rising equity returns,
investors are sitting on sizable, unrealized capital gains. The bad news: Market gains
can feel like a financial straitjacket, making it difficult to rebalance portfolios without
triggering significant tax liabilities.

Against this backdrop, now is the time for investors to review where the assets are
held—distinguishing between taxable and tax-advantaged accounts, and the attendant tax
ramifications. The appropriate mix depends on individual circumstances, including future
2 “Investors seek shelter from heightened exposure to AI”, The Financial Times, October 6, 2026.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
withdrawal taxes. It is also time to consider tax-loss strategies, charitable donations of
securities and other tactics and strategies since in the end, the measure of inv estment
success isn’t simply how much money investors make. It’s how much they keep.
Number Four: Is it time to take profits?* There is a peculiarity to bull markets: The
more money investors make, the more the attendant tax liabilities. Tax liabilities, in turn,

can discourage selling, and the reluctance to sell can leave investors dangerously exposed
to yesterday’s leaders and compound concentration risks. That brings up an emotionally
difficult decision: When should investors sell their leaders or take some chips off the table?
The question is notably relevant today, given four years of substantial Equity gains.
Beyond tax considerations, investors need to understand that profit-taking can create
financial flexibility. Realized after-tax gains can replenish cash reserves, fund near-

term spending needs, reduce leverage or most importantly, provide capital to rebalance
portfolios in other areas of the market. In general, the goal of taking profits isn’t to get
out of the market—it is to ensure that after years of accumulated gains, these proceeds
are redeployed and redistributed across various asset classes that continue to support
and underpin long-term wealth accumulation.
Number Five: How well is my portfolio positioned for a new interest rate

regime? For much of the past quarter century, investors operated in a world of copious
liquidity, declining interest rates and relatively cheap capital. The era of capital abundance
favored stocks over bonds, but that era is over—we are shifting to a world of capital
scarcity, or intense competition for capital and the attendant hike in the cost of capital.
In this world, higher rates weigh the most on long-duration assets. Positioning for this
regime favors companies with strong balance sheets and cash flow over firms with heavy

debt or refinancing needs. High-quality bonds are more attractiv e in this environment;
sovereign debt risks are concentrated more overseas (i.e., France) than the U.S.
Number Six: How well is my portfolio positioned for the next shock? Shocks
happen. If the past four years have taught investors anything, it is that the unexpected
should be the expected. This decade alone has produced gut-wrenching market mo ves
related to a global pandemic, a ground war in Europe, the reconfiguration of global

supply chains, epic technological change and a war in the Middle East. Through it all, U.S.
Equities have trended higher but hardly in a linear fashion.
The next shocks could emerge in the global bond market, where mounting go vernment
debt and higher borrowing costs have triggered mounting volatility. It could emerge from
disappointing returns on massive AI investments. Or it could come from a geopolitical
event, another energy shock or any other unforeseen event. Investors can’t predict the

next shock, but they can prepare for it. So now is the time for investors to stress test
their portfolios against a potential shock, examining how a steep downdraft in Equities
or spike in bond yields would affect their retirement plans, liquidity needs and other
financial goals and objectives.
Exhibit 2: Six Questions to Stress Test Your Portfolio.
The six questions What investors should examine Portfolio diagnostic
1. How concentrated am I? Mega-cap technology exposure and overlapping holdings % of portfolio in top 10 stocks

2. Am I truly diversified? Asset allocation, geographic mix and correlated exposures Current equity/bond mix versus target
3. How tax-efficient am I? Unrealized gains, asset location and tax-loss harvesting Embedded capital gains and estimated tax cost of rebalancing
4. Is it time to take profits? Portfolio drift, oversized winners and valuations Positions exceeding target allocation
5. Am I ready for higher interest rates? Duration, leverage, refinancing risks and cash flows Bond duration and exposure to highly leveraged companies

6. Am I prepared for the next shock? Liquidity, downside exposure and portfolio resilience Estimated loss in a 20% equity selloff
Source: Chief Investment Office. Data as of October 22, 2026.
* “Pop the Champagne,” Portfolio Insights, Chief Investment Office, August 2026.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are rising credit spreads signaling about the broader market environment?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Rising French bond yields with a sharp rise in spreads above German bunds, mixed
with AI buildout concerns, are creating nervousness across the capital markets. This
nervousness centers around the ability to fund deficits, the potential for slower growth

and the future return on the announced capital investment. We expect these concerns
to continue to linger well into next year as growth worries remain in France, bond
yields stay sticky around current levels, and, in the case of AI, investors demand more
compensation for financing.
At this stage, we do not expect the rising spreads in targeted areas to be a precursor
for a material slowdown in economic growth overall. Rather, we believe this is a natural

signal that investors are moving up the quality stack as we move into the middle of
the AI buildout. The bottom line is that rising credit spreads in lower quality corners of
the market are natural along the lifecycle of financing. When free cash flow is called
into question, or when debt financing costs begin to approach the expected return on
investment, investors begin to reprice risk.
We continue to emphasize higher quality balance sheets in corporate and sovereign

credit as well as higher quality leaders in the AI buildout overall. The closer we get to
2027, investors will likely begin to transition from too much exposure to the “buildout”
and not enough exposure to “AI adopters.”

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
SS&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and
services.
S&P 500 Market-cap Weighted Index is a float-adjusted market-capitalization-weighted index, meaning a company's influence and percentage weight in the index are determined by its
total market value (share price multiplied by the number of outstanding shares).

S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is a variation of the traditional S&P 500 where every single company in the index is given the exact same weight—0.2%—regardless of its size or market
capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Industrials Index imposes equal weights on the index constituents included in the S&P 500 that are classified in the GICS® industrials sector.
Russell 1000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of the 1,000 largest publicly traded companies in the United States.

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 283: De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-09-15
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
De-reservification, Not De-dollarization
The world isn’t moving away from the dollar. It is shifting its dollars from traditional
reserves to state banks, pension funds, and other quasi-sovereign investors.
(Bernardo Montoya/Courtesy Reuters)
PUBLISHED
September 15, 2026 8:37 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
This is a joint blog with Alex Etra, Senior Macro
Strategist and head of Flow of Funds at Vanda
Research.
The IMF’s data on the currency composition of
foreign exchange (FX) reserves is scrutinized carefully
for any signs that the world is shifting way from the
dollar.
Yet it doesn’t really matter that much if the dollar’s
share of reserves has shifted from 56.5 percent to 57
percent. The ink-to-impact ratio of the quarterly

reporting on the latest COFER data is all off.
Neither the stock of global reserves nor the stock of
dollar reserves has changed much in the last ten years.
The action is elsewhere.

### Page 3

3
China’s state banks (per the Bank of International
Settlements) have almost as many foreign assets as the
central bank (PBOC).
Japan’s Government Pension Investment Fund
(GPIF) has almost as many foreign assets ($986
billion) as the government has FX reserves ($1.1
trillion at the end of August). Japan’s FX reserves are
on the books of the Ministry of Finance and the
GPIF is overseen by the Ministry of Health, Welfare
and Labor—so these are almost all assets of the

Government of Japan or the broader public sector,
not its central bank.
Korea’s National Pension Service has more foreign
assets than the Bank of Korea has FX reserves.
A different dynamic is in play in Taiwan, as the
government doesn’t run a big retirement fund. But in

### Page 4

4
a world where the hedge ratio of the life insurers has
been (and still is) used as a policy tool, the shift in
Taiwan’s foreign asset accumulation away from the
Central Bank of China (CBC) and over to
institutions whose activities in the foreign currency
market it directly influences broadly fits into the same
theme. So too does the PBOC’s use of the
“macroprudential adjustment factor” effectively adjust
banks’ net foreign assets in much the same way that

CBC regulations adjust lifer hedge ratios.
In the last year, the lifers’ hedge ratios—and their
direct hedges with the central bank—have been one
of the key tools the CBC has used to manage the
Taiwan dollar (and guard the lifers’ solvency).
Asia’s big surplus economies are in a sense just
catching up with the world’s oil exporters. Norway
put its oil surplus into a sovereign wealth fund from
the start. Kuwait, Qatar, and Abu Dhabi (the most
oil-rich emirate) all also have large sovereign funds.

And the world’s biggest oil exporter has joined them:
The Public Investment Fund has almost as many
foreign assets as the Saudi Central Bank (SAMA).*
What’s more, the available evidence suggests that the
dollar share of these pools is in line with or higher
than the dollar share of global FX reserves (it depends
on the institution and jurisdiction).

### Page 5

5
In other words, by looking at the (easily available)
data on the world’s static holdings of formal FX
reserve assets, scholars and analysts miss most of the
growth in the world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign
foreign assets.
Consider China.
No serious analyst now disputes that China’s state
banks—including the policy banks (the China
Development Bank, China Exim)—hold several
trillion in foreign assets.
China reports $3.3 trillion in gross foreign assets to

the BIS (the net position, counting foreign bank
claims on the entire Chinese economy not just the
banks, is $2.5 trillion). That maps to the BOP data,
which shows almost $4 trillion in gross outflows
through the banking system (technically, the sum of
gross outflows in “other” plus the $500 billion in
foreign currency bonds held by the state commercial
banks)

### Page 6

6
Since 2010 (and even more so after 2014) the net
outflow through the state banks has exceeded reserve
accumulation.
The broad contours of this story are confirmed by the
balance sheet data reported by state commercial
banks in their 2025 annual reports, which showed

### Page 7

7
that the top five banks held a combined $2.5 trillion
in foreign currency assets (mostly held abroad).
The Chinese haven’t disclosed the foreign assets of
the two policy banks (with at this stage the complicity
of the IMF, which has neither analyzed the role of
SAFE policy bank financing, nor highlighted the
glaring gap in China’s own reporting). But the work
of AidData points to nearly $1 trillion in foreign
assets, with a hefty dollar share.
The available data sources all suggest that the bulk of

banks—puts the dollar share of their offshore foreign
currency assets at around 70 percent. That is well
above the last disclosed dollar share of China’s formal
reserves (55 percent in 2019). The banks’ annual

### Page 8

8
reports suggest that about 70 percent of the banks’
assets are in dollars and Hong Kong dollars (55
percent USD/15 percent HKD)—a bit below the
dollar and Hong Kong dollar share of their liabilities
(swaps out of the HKD fund a decent share of the
banks euro assets).
Put simply, SAFE’s static dollar holdings aren’t the
important story.
The real story is the rapid growth of the state banks
dollar holdings.
Japan holds a high (though undisclosed) dollar share

in its FX reserves, and those reserves are primarily
invested in Treasuries—so Japan’s MoF is now clearly
the largest contributor to the U.S. data on foreign
official holdings of Treasuries. (SAFE has shifted its
funds out of U.S. custodians, and thus increasingly
appears in the data as a “private” holder in a European
custodial center)
Japan’s formal reserves haven’t changed much in the
last ten years; periodic sales to limit the yen’s weakness
have offset most of the MoF’s accumulated interest

income. Foreign currency reserves were $1.2 trillion
in 2014 and $1.16 trillion at the end of Q1. They
dipped to about $1 trillion at the end of August after
sales of close to $100 billion at the end of July/start of
August.

### Page 9

9
What has grown is the foreign portfolio of the GPIF.
it was around $400 billion in 2014, ~$1 trillion in Q1
of 2026.
GPIF’s foreign bonds aren’t held exclusively in dollars,
but roughly 52 percent of its foreign bonds (26
percent of all bonds) are USD-denominated.
But the GPIF, like most international investors, holds
a large share of its equity portfolio in the U.S. (~$165
billion, 65 percent of the foreign equity portfolio).
Korea is a more extreme version of Japan.

The Bank of Korea’s FX reserves have been roughly
$375-450 billion over the last ten years or so. Around
two-thirds of those are now in dollars (as of the end of
2025).

### Page 10

10
From 2012 to 2025, the foreign assets at the NPS
have grown from ~$80 billion (around 21 percent of
total assets) to over $660 billion (55 percent of total
assets)—and something like 70 percent of those are in
dollars. 76 percent of the fund’s equities are in dollars,
and 66 percent of its debt. And while a lower share of
its “alternative” portfolio is in North America, most
alternative mandates are awarded in dollars.

### Page 11

11
In other words, the majority of the dollar assets held
by Korea’s government (the NPS reports to the
Ministry of Health and Welfare, and the Minister of
Health chairs its asset allocation committee) aren’t
held by the central bank and aren’t in the IMF’s
COFER data set.
Taiwan is an unusual case, as the life insurers aren’t
technically sovereign investors. But with their
regulator now explicitly encouraging the them to hold
an unhedged foreign currency book and hence they

now rely on the state for their solvency, they have a bit
of an official character (a significant appreciation of
the TWD would blow through their volatility reserves
and leave them with fewer assets than liabilities, as
their foreign assets are largely held against TWD
policies). The CBC certainly now uses the lifers desire
to unwind existing hedges to help manage the Taiwan

### Page 12

12
dollar. And they have an exceptionally high dollar
share in their foreign assets—over 95 percent.
The Saudi Public Investment Fund (PIF) is another
example.
It keeps roughly $200 billion of its $250 billion in
foreign investments in dollars—well above the U.S.
share of a standard global stock index. Almost all of
the PIF’s $30 billion in external debt is also in dollars.
And the PIF now has only slightly fewer foreign
assets than the Saudi Central Bank has reserves.**

The broad story is thus pretty clear: the growth in the
world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign assets is not
coming through an increase in FX reserve holdings
managed by the world’s central banks.

### Page 13

13
And, in the critical case of China, the dollar share of
China’s quasi-sovereign foreign assets is likely higher
than the dollar share of their formal FX reserves.
Adam Tooze has noted that inflows into the U.S. are
now coming from investors looking for a profit, not
investors looking for safety. What he calls the “profit
dollar” manifests itself in a number of different ways.
More inflows into corporate bonds and equities for
one. Less official demand for Treasuries for another.

More opacity too.
Those same trends are at play among sovereign and
quasi-sovereign investors.
So don’t obsess about the dollar’s chare in formal FX
reserves. Do recognize that the dollar’s “reserve
currency role” isn’t the source of any significant new
inflows into the dollar.

### Page 14

14
The dollar’s dominant role in the international
monetary system depends on much more than the
size and composition of central bank FX reserves. It is
as much, perhaps more so (given that FX reserve
managers are themselves ultimately liability matchers)
a function of the portfolio choice of a set of
private/semi-private/quasi-sovereign investors that
are much more difficult to observe. Reserve currency
status is not only about FX reserves in a strict sense.

That’s been true for some time. Most of the current
flow into U.S. from the “official” sector is coming
from investors who are not classic reserve managers.
Those flows remain heavily tilted toward the dollar, at
least for now.***
And any real de-dollarization would likely occur first
among these investors.
Put differently, the dollar’s global role is increasingly
as a source of returns, not a source of safety. The
foreign bid, private and public, is for risk, not for

Treasuries. That doesn’t help Scott Bessent much
right now, but it has helped keep valuations in the
stock market extended. And the global debate on the
dollar’s role lags the evolution in the dollar’s role, and
the risks associated with that new role.
It is currently fashionable in some circles to point to
the diminution of the “convenience yield” on

### Page 15

15
Treasuries even while the U.S. dollar continues to
enjoy such a privilege. But the shift in the global
investor base, and their apparent portfolio preference
for U.S. risk assets in lieu of Treasuries, may help
explain the divergence between the convenience yield
on UST and the convenience yield on USD.
And of course, if the negative convenience yield
implies that Treasuries are trading at a historical
discount, the persistence of the latter—and its

concentration in risk assets—suggests that the “profit
dollar” itself is likely trading at a historical premium.
That apparent premium on U.S. risk assets deserves as
much (or more) scrutiny as the currency composition
of central bank FX reserves.
* The PIF and Saudi Aramco also now have a certain
amount of debt.
** Reserves (now mostly in Treasuries, so should
appear in the TIC data, unlike at some times in the
past.

### Page 16

16
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.

share is below the likely dollar share of Japan’s MoF.

## Document 311: Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP .  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

### Page 2

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

### Page 4

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

another hike in March, but either way we see renewed cuts in late 2027/early 2028.

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP , and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP , although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.
